# Review — 063-oversized-artifacts-warn-with-a-fix

## Summary

Five passes over 063's window since its reopen (diff-base cc25ee6e, base-source reopen) to 095a7dad.

What the window holds:

- 063's own change: tasks 15 and 16 (12ea0e44, 13096627), the 063 part of f395900f, and the sweeps of 063's directory.
- The commits naming no spec that the window holds:
  - b1d319f1, dropping Auggie, Antigravity and OpenCode;
  - 0ce70cd0, the Rust 1.100.0 toolchain;
  - the lockfile updates, e3e6eea9 and seven earlier;
  - the CI pins, f0d0cf49 and eight earlier;
  - 0afc0c00, Pi's prompt copy;
  - the docs(agents) edits.

Who read what:

- Eight reviewers split the scope by behavior, and each read the nine loaded rule files whole:
  - 063's own change;
  - b1d319f1's host and session layer;
  - b1d319f1's install, manifest and permission primitives;
  - the toolchain, lockfile and CI chores, with runtime/CHANGELOG.md and AGENTS.md;
  - the bootstrap and its procedure;
  - b1d319f1's framework and docs side;
  - b1d319f1's scripts side, with the analyze sources;
  - runtime/CHANGELOG.md lines 4101-5015.
- A ninth reviewer read no rule file, so its pass is not used. Three of the others re-ran its entries.

Coverage:

- examined counts the 69 of 91 scope entries a pass read whole:
  - runtime/CHANGELOG.md counts once, read whole between two reviewers, lines 1-4100 and 4101-5015;
  - AGENTS.md and framework/constitution.md count from the copies loaded from disk at HEAD, confirmed unmodified.
- unread names the other 22:
  - 14 large runtime files, read by the window's hunks and named ranges: runtime/src/interpreter/mod.rs, interpreter/payload.rs, main.rs, schema/descriptions.rs and schema/primitives.rs; runtime/src/primitives/rewrite_spec_links.rs, apply_manifest.rs, check_artifacts.rs, check_corpus_links.rs, dashboard.rs, host_config.rs, merge_managed_block.rs and merge_permissions.rs; and runtime/tests/exec_subprocess.rs;
  - 8 generated mirrors, diffed against sources a pass read whole but not read themselves: .claude/commands/ductus/analyze.md, audit.md and configure.md; .ductus/reference/analyze.md and audit.md; and .pi/prompts/ductus-analyze.md, ductus-audit.md and ductus-configure.md.

Fixed in the run:

- Two QUAL-CLAIM-001 SHOULDs, which a reviewer filed as low confidence and the host judged high, since all three of the rule's conditions hold:
  - scripts/audit/runtime-hardcoded-paths.sh read an empty or unreadable runtime/src as a clean scan;
  - scripts/audit/host-namespace-parity.sh exited silently with nothing to compare.
  - Each now reports what it examined, and each was proved on a fixture to fire.
- Four chores, the observations marked fixed. The new check-artifact-size CLI test fails with the skipped clause removed from size_check_blocks.

Not fixed:

- BE-PAYLOAD-001 on check-artifact-size's result stays low confidence. Its Verification judges a spec or plan's size commitment, and 063's analysis judges that.

Stored decisions:

- Nine matched and stand.
- The one on the bootstrap's curls expired: every executable curl there now carries --proto, --tlsv1.2 and both timeouts, as runtime-acquisition.yml's do.
- The dashboard walk's stored waiver stands on its reason, though dashboard now takes 0.53 s, best of 3, on 76 feature directories, against the 0.23-0.25 s recorded. The walks' share of that was not re-measured.

1 fix round(s): 1 re-read, 0 diff-checked, 0 exempt, 0 not re-read.

## MUST violations (blocking)

*None.*

## SHOULD violations (advisory)

*None.*

## Low-confidence findings

### LOW-CONFIDENCE: BE-PAYLOAD-001 — check-artifact-size returns variable-size lists with no stated maximum response size

- **File**: `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifact_size.rs:100-188`
- **Rule**: Response payloads MUST have a bounded maximum size. Endpoints that return collections able to exceed a small fixed maximum MUST paginate per `BE-PAGE` (api-backend.md) rather than return an unbounded list in a single response.
- **Finding**: The result's `oversized` (one ~1 KB record with warning, message and fixes per over-threshold subject, one per scenario file), `skipped` (one per unreadable subject) and `notices` (one per unknown [artifacts] key) grow with the spec's file count; `project-files` (4) and `fixes` (<=3 per record) are fixed. Neither 063's spec, plan nor data-model commits to a maximum size or to the [artifacts] read-size-bytes bound spec 080 gave every result that grows with the corpus; 080's research table classified this primitive as not growing. The growth is bounded only by one spec directory's scenario count, so confidence is low. Predates the window (the lists are 063's original shape); task 15 added only the fixed four-record project half.
- **Auto-fixable**: no
- **Suggested fix**: Either state in 063's plan/data-model that the result is bounded by one spec's subject files and name the bound, or route `oversized`/`skipped` through bounded_lists.rs (the file form past [artifacts] read-size-bytes) as 080 does for corpus-growing results.

## Waived findings

*None.*

## Deferred findings

*None.*

Predates the window. — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md:203` — **waived**: Spec 065's decision (a fork override, announced on stderr and in the run); the failure mode is a loud 404 halt, not a silent fallback, and the variable is operator-controlled rather than user-supplied, so BE-INPUT-007's untrusted-URL boundary does not apply. (decided by the rule)
- other — b1d319f1 edited three already-shipped migrations in place (ductus-rename 0.28.0, runtime-store-path 0.28.0, session-file-consolidate 0.10.0), dropping their Auggie/Antigravity/OpenCode steps and `opencode.json` from runtime-store-path's target_paths; an adopter still on a dropped host who first crosses these migrations now gets no rewrite. Deliberate (the hosts are unsupported and `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` prints a leftover-tree notice) and unexercised by a real adopter run, which 012 task 12 already holds open as an unchecked task. — `framework/migrations/ductus-rename.md:1` — **waived**: Pipeline-machinery observation on framework work: the removal is the chore's intent, a leftover-tree notice covers the stranded adopter, and the unexercised-migration risk is already a tracked unchecked task (specs/012-multi-agent-govern/tasks.md task 12), so it is deferred where the pipeline surfaces it and nothing new is created. (decided by the rule)
- other — §spec-lifecycle resolves the plan rule against the shared test only by sentence order — `framework/constitution.md`. Re-checked: the window's only hunk in this file is the one-line Host integration edit (drops Auggie / Antigravity / OpenCode prefixes), and the file is unmodified at HEAD, so the stored issue's text is unchanged and nothing in the window resolves or worsens it. — `framework/constitution.md` — **waived**: coherent as read, the rule sitting between the three cases and Anything else, and the section is held at its byte ceiling
- other — scripts/tests/test-install.sh's header Coverage list stops at V (and omits L2), while cases W (an agent key outside the registry halts) and X (claude seeds settings when absent and keeps an existing file) were added by b1d319f1; the header is the file's own index of its cases. — `scripts/tests/test-install.sh:6-33` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — analyze step 17 and the reference render only `oversized`, `skipped` and `notices`; the result's `project-files` states (absent, measured) reach neither the report nor the record, so a project with no AGENTS.md or events.md reads the same as one whose files were measured and found small. 063's scenario requires only that the result report absence, which it does. — `framework/commands/analyze.md:17` — **waived**: Scenario project-files-are-measured places 'absent is reported' in the primitive's result, which holds; rendering it in the analyze report is a design choice about the pipeline's own machinery, not a break of 063's contract, and a not-mechanical edit. (decided by the rule)
- other — the `configure_retired` dict in permission-entry-shape.sh's embedded python holds one key (claude) and is looped over with `if key in seeds else []` guards left from the multi-agent form. — `scripts/audit/permission-entry-shape.sh:333-343` — **waived**: Behavior is correct and the guard still serves a registry without a claude row; a simplification of audit machinery, not a defect. (decided by the rule)
- other — install.sh validates the agent key only in its final case arm, after both network requests, so each of test W's five removed-agent keys fetches the bootstrap before halting; the test asserts the message and that nothing was written, not the request count that tests O and P assert for a bad --ref. — `scripts/tests/test-install.sh:380-388` — **waived**: install.sh is not in this agent's entries and halting before a write is the contract the test holds; early validation is an improvement to the installer, not a defect in this chore. (decided by the rule)
- convention — runtime/CHANGELOG.md line 4101 (the 0.29.4 entry's opening line, a released note) names another project: "the class of defect the real `papur` bootstrap surfaced twice". AGENTS.md Workflow forbids recording another project's name in this repository and says to describe the shape instead. Predates the window (introduced by 1a5bf19f, a 027/022 commit). — `runtime/CHANGELOG.md:4101` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
